and making the other arrangements for these OMWs.
Evaluations by those attending:
A standard evaluation sheet was circulated to each participant at the
time that he registered. During the day, the registrants were encouraged to fill
the sheets out. The committee made a particular effort to collect these
evaluations by standing at the exit door at the end of the program and collecting
them as the registrants left.
Numbers of evaluation sheets received:
San Jose – 50
Santa Barbara – 34
Eureka – 18
Stockton – 44
San Diego – 35
Anaheim – 33
The evaluations were almost always positive. While time and space do
not allow a complete description of the responses to each entry, the summary
question (“My overall impression of the workshop is…”) was usually answered
with a response that indicated that the participant believed that his time was well
spent and/or that he approved of the activity. It is to be appreciated that a
couple of Masons so enjoyed one of these that they attended a second one
during 2003.

Financial considerations:
The table that accompanies this report represents the information I have
collected. These data do not include the Grand Lodge costs of pre-OMW
mailings nor the administrative support in San Francisco. It is also possible that
other committees paid for some of the participants, although I have tried to
discourage this practice. (If a committeeman from another committee is
conducting one or more of the breakout sessions at a particular OMW, the

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

325

OMW Committee should be responsible for paying his expenses.)
It would appear from the table that there was a $900.00 excess of
income over expenses. However, this is not necessarily an accurate reflection of
the financial situation. As noted, some funding support may have come from
other committees. Furthermore, not all of the speakers submitted expense
requests, and at least in some of those instances, it is known that the individual
speakers simply paid their expenses out of their own pockets.

Future possible programmatic changes:
The topic of fund-raising may have been less relevant to these younger
officers, as that was the one that appeared to receive a number of lukewarm
evaluations. On a personal note, I have also wondered why we are providing so
much financial information to the younger officers (the intended target
audience). Does a junior officer really have to know about the various forms
(financial reporting) or the details of investing? Two topics that have not been
covered that may be of more relevance to these men are information about the
youth groups and assistance with public speaking.
As of the time of preparation of this report, the future of the OMWs as
currently constituted is unclear. Apparently the Grand Lodge Executive
Committee wants the leadership education program to be changed to include
senior as well as junior officers, to consist of single topic sessions (with ultimate
certification), and to be expanded in size. (This latter goal would, of necessity,
prohibit sessions in smaller communities such as Eureka; this may create some
sense of disenfranchisement in those areas.) In the past, the OMWs have been
scheduled so as not to conflict with Grand Lodge activities in the same area.
However, there now appears to be some desire for Grand Lodge participation in
these training sessions.
The OMWs have achieved name recognition throughout the
jurisdiction. Even if the contents of the program change, I would strongly
advise that the name be kept.

Personnel for reappointment to the committee next year
As permanent members of this committee, I would recommend the
following individuals: Bruce Abanathie, Russ Charvonia, Ron Cooper, Gene
Goldman, Lee Hughes, Levon Keshishian, Scott Nave, Richard Pumerantz,
Larry Reynolds, Hal Scott, and Al Swanson. John Adams has asked to be
excused because of other responsibilities that he will be assuming, and we will
need someone to handle arrangements in the South. I have not asked a specific
person yet, as it is not yet determined how next year’s program is going to be
managed. As a suggestion, I would put forth the name of Dan Sola in San

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

326

Diego.

Concluding observations:
It has been with a great deal of pleasure that I have had the opportunity
to serve as chairman of the OMW Committee for the year 2003. Overall, it
would appear that the six workshops were all well received and successful. The
current fee structure would appear to be adequate, particularly if some of the
speakers continue to absorb their own expenses. If we are going to make this
project totally self-sufficient, however, it may be necessary to raise the fee
(perhaps to $30) and avoid any extraordinary expenses. In this latter regard, I
would suggest that we not return to the Scottish Rite facility in San Jose, as the
rent and food expenses are obviously far out of touch with what we pay
elsewhere. Similarly, the food expenses in Anaheim would need to be lowered,
but this may be accomplished by not participating in the breakfast sales that
occurred this year.
Respectfully submitted,
Ronald L. Koretz, Chairman
Ronald E. Cooper
Levon H. Keshishian
Robert A. Reynolds

Leelon E. Hughes
Larry A. Reynolds, Sr.
Hal G. Scott
Domingo R. Sola

REPORT OF THE COMMITTEE ON
TRIAL REVIEW
To the Most Worshipful Grand Lodge, F.& A.M. of California:
Since the 2002 Annual Communication there have been referred to
your Committee on Trial Review 10 completed and three (3) applications for
restorations from expulsion. In addition, there were four (4) cases which were
disposed of before Trial and six (6) cases pending but now completed and
submitted to the Grand Lodge for review.
Your committee has reviewed each of these Trial records numbered
2446, and 2451 through 2459 and finds that in each there was substantial
compliance with the procedural requirements of our constitution and ordinances
and the result is supported in the record of each case and there are no
miscarriage of justice.
Resolved that in each of the Trial records numbered 2446, 2451, 2454,
2455, 2456, 2457, and 2458 the judgment and penalty imposed be ratified,
affirmed and approved.

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

327

Trial records 2452, 2453 and 2459 will be reported on separately.
Your committee thanks the various Inspectors, Judge Donald Fretz, the
Grand Secretary and his staff particularly Pamela Lindsten, Administrative
Assistant as well as the Grand Master for their efforts to resolve these conflicts
without a Masonic Trial. Each of us again is reminded of our obligation as
Masons to whisper wise council in the ear of an offending brother instead of
being eager to run forward with Masonic Charges. If peace and harmony are to
prevail in our lodges, each of us must practice our Masonic Tenets.
Trial Record No. 2452
The accused was charged with unmasonic conduct, with 3
specifications: 1) The commission of the crime of petty theft; 2) The
commission of the crime of possession of a controlled narcotic substance; and,
3) Falsifying his application for the degrees of Masonry. The Trial was held in
Tujunga Lodge No. 592, on the 8th of February 2003, before a panel of
commissioners, and presided over by a trial master. The evidence established
that the accused was convicted of petty theft and possession of a controlled
substance in a court of law. At the Masonic Trial, the commissioners found the
accused guilty of all 3 specifications, but not guilty of unmasonic conduct.
The Trial Review Committee concluded that the penalty of no
unmasonic conduct was inconsistent with the facts.
It is therefore, recommended that the penalty be raised to expulsion.
Trial Record No. 2453
Your committee on Trial Review recommends that this case be sent
back for the following reasons: The decision in this case should be reversed.

1. Conduct a further investigation to determine what the charges were to
support charge II of the information.
2. Investigate the basis for the Probation Violation to determine if it
would be basis for an additional count for charges of unmasonic.
3. Amend the charges and list the facts on which count 2 of the
information was based or word the charges to more correctly reflect the
conviction.
4. Send back the charges upon Brother Gonzales and have the certificate
of service of reflect the source of the address.

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

328

Trial Record No. 2459
Subject was arrested on 2 felony counts, violations of Section 288A of
the Penal Code, subject pled guilty to two misdemeanor counts of molesting a
child under the age of 14.

Definition of Unmasonic Conduct: Page 2
Manual for Masonic Charges and Trials
B. The following breaches of faith or moral turpitude constitutes unmasonic
conduct sufficient to support the filing of charges against a Mason.
1.

Any violation before or after initiation of the Criminal Laws of the
United States or any other governmental entity involving Moral
Turpitude.

Subject was arrested, charged and found guilty of Section 647.6 P.C.
He is a registered sex offender under the Megan’s Law.
At a Masonic Trial held on 7 July, 2003 subject was found guilty on
counts 1 and 2 and the penalty set at “reprimand in open lodge.
Your committee on Trial Review recommends that the penalty be set
aside and instead be raised to expulsion.
Resolved that in Trial Record No. 2459 the judgment and
penalty imposed be set and that the penalty be raised to
expulsion.

Thomas C. Letcher, Chairman

REPORT OF THE COMMITTEE ON
U.S. CONSTITUTION OBSERVANCE
To the Most Worshipful Grand Lodge, F.& A.M. of California:
This year was the last year of our Committee as a separate entity and
this has not hampered our efforts in the least. We have continued to promote
the enlightenment, and education of the communities regarding our Heritage by
expanding our activities to include any Patriotic Holiday that an individual
lodge may choose to celebrate with an excellent response.

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

329

Many Lodges chose Memorial Day for their focus, others
Independence Day and still others Labor Day. The celebrations included
picnics, barbeques, school assemblies, public speakers at civic gatherings and
more. Over 30 Lodges participated in “I Signed the Constitution” ceremonies,
utilizing materials from the National Constitution Center in Philadelphia. Still
others used materials, film and booklets from the National Center for
Constitution Studies in Idaho. Negotiations from the Chair of the Committee
resulted in obtaining Constitution Booklets for a nominal fee of 25 cents each, a
substantial savings from the usual $1.00 per booklet fee, making it feasible for a
Lodge to adopt one or more fifth grades in their areas and provide these
materials to the students at no charge to the schools.
As reports roll in from the individual Lodges, certificates will be
forthcoming and recognition will be given to worthy Lodges and individuals.
In the ensuing year, we will be faced with the re-organization of the
committee in accordance with the resolves of the 152nd session of the Grand
Lodge proceedings and we will continue to promote the enlightenment and
education of our communities in the principles that made our country what it is
today, the symbol of Freedom to the rest of the world and its oppressed citizens
everywhere.
Respectfully submitted
B. Eugene Collins, Chairman
Michael J. Bear
Mario Chiappe

NAVIGATION TIPS

Scroll through the document using
scroll bars on the side of the window or
the page navigation tool at the bottom.

Click on any of the items in the bookmark
section of the document and you’ll navigate
directly to that page or section.
You can then move to the next page using the
ARROW KEYS on your keyboard.
Return to the beginning of the document at any
time by clicking on the Table of Contents
Bookmark.
If you want to enlarge or reduce the size of the
document, click on the Zoom In/Zoom Out
bookmarks.
Navigate through the document
by clicking on any of the items in
the Table of Contents or the
Index.
The “Hand Tool” will change to
an “Index Finger”…click on the
line and you’ll move to the
desired page.
To get back, click on the Table
of Contents/Index bookmarks.
